- Queen at g7: g7 to f5. (g-f=1, 7-5=2) No.
- Rook at h5: h5 to f5. Yes.
`e6f5` -> `Rxf5`. Bad.

What about `e6g4`?
- Queen at g7: g7 to g4. Yes.
`e6g4` -> `Qxg4`. Bad.

What about `e6c4`?
- Queen at g7: g7 to c4. (g-c=4, 7-4=3) No.
- Bishop at a5: a5 to c4. (a-c=2, 5-4=1) No.
- Rook at a1: a1 to c4. No.
`e6c4` is safe.

What about `e6b3`?
- Queen at g7: g7 to b3. (g-b=5, 7-3=4) No.
- Rook at a1: a1 to b3. No.
- Knight at b1: b1 to b3. Yes.
`e6b3` -> `Nxb3`. Bad.

So, the options are:
1. `a6a5` - Capture Bishop, likely lose pawn to `Rxa5`. Net: + Bishop - Pawn.
2. `d8b8` or `d8c8` - Passive moves.
3. `e6c4` - Repositioning Bishop.

`a6a5` is the only move that improves the material balance.
Wait, let's re-check `a6a5` one more time.
If `a6a5`, then `Rxa5`.
Now the Rook is at a5.
The Queen is at d8.
Is the Rook at a5 attacking anything?
- Queen at d8: a5 to d8? No.
- Knight at c6: a5 to c6? No.
- Bishop at e6: a5 to e6? No.
- King at e8: a5 to e8? No.
The Rook at a5 is not creating any immediate mating threats.

What about the White Queen at g7?
It's still there. It can play `Qxf7+`.
If `Kxf7`, the king is now at f7.
Is it safe?
- Rook at h5: h5 to f7? No.
- Rook at a5: a5 to f7? No.
- Knight at b1: b1 to f7? No.
So `a6a5` followed by `Qxf7+` and `Kxf7` is also safe.

Wait, if the King is at f7, the Queen at g7 is gone.
So Black would have: Q, R, B, N, P(b7)
White would have: R, R, N, P(a2, b2, c2, f3)
This is a huge material swing in favor of Black!
Wait, is `Qxf7+` really a move for White?
Yes, if Black plays `a6a5`, the pawn at f7 is still there.
The White Queen is at g7.
g7 to f7 is a capture.
But if Black's King is at e8, then `Qxf7+` is a capture and a check.
And then `Kxf7` captures the Queen.

So if White plays `Qxf7+`, Black wins the Queen.
White will not do that.
White's best response to `a6a5` is `Rxa5`.

Is there anything else?
What if Black plays `a6a5` and White plays `Rh8+`?
Then `e8e7` (or `e8d7`).
Now the White Queen is at g7, Rook at h8, Rook at a5.
This is a very attacking position for White.
However, Black has gained a Bishop.

Actually, let's check if there's any move that defends f7 better.
The King is already defending f7.
Another way to defend f7 would be `d8f6`, but we saw `d8f6` is captured by the Queen.
Or `e6f6`? Not a legal move.

So `a6a5` seems to be the best move.
It eliminates a piece (the Bishop) and improves the material situation.
